- For negative lag -d, first index is smaller. If k smaller, p as second: -delta*h_k. If p smaller, p as first: +delta*(1-h_k).

C[+d] += delta* (1-h_k) if p>k else -delta*h_k
C[-d] += -delta*h_k if p>k else delta*(1-h_k)

Let's design binary local search in detail.

Data structures:
- h: np.ndarray dtype float64 or int8. Use float64 for updates? int8 with p? Use np.float64 for C, h for vector operations.
- C: float64 length 2n-1, initialized by recompute (or efficient from h). We'll recompute from scratch at start.
- M = n-1.
- dx=2/n.
- current_max_count = C.max() (float, integer).
- maybe W weights: We can skip weights for simplicity and use max C. But weighted helps. Let's implement without weights first; if time, add.

Move update function:
```
def apply_flip(C, p, delta):
    n=len(C)//2+1? Actually C length 2n-1, M=n-1.
    if p>0:
       left = h[:p]
       d = p - np.arange(p) # int positive
       idxp = M + d
       idxn = M - d
       C[idxp] += delta*(1-left)
       C[idxn] -= delta*left
    if p+1<n:
       right = h[p+1:]
       d = np.arange(1, n-p)
       idxp = M+d
       idxn = M-d
       C[idxp] -= delta*right
       C[idxn] += delta*(1-right)
    # if binary, C[M] unchanged; if fractional update:
    old = h[p]; new = old+delta
    C[M] += new*(1-new) - old*(1-old) # for p as both? Wait update formulas for s=0: corr[0] = ∑h_i(1-h_i). Change as that. But if we apply flip sequentially for binary, C[M] unchanged; formula okay.
    h[p]=new
```

Count of 1->0 at lag d can be popcount(ones & ~(ones<<d)). That is O(n/word) but Python big int bit operations in C, very fast! This is a great idea. For binary h, represent set of ones as Python int bitmask. For each d, count positive = popcount(ones_mask >>? & ~ones shifted). Let's define ones_mask bit i=1 if h_i=1. For d>0, C[M+d] (one later, zero earlier) = count i=0..n-d-1 with bit i+d=1, bit i=0. This is sum over j=d..n-1 bit_j=1 and bit_{j-d}=0. Let shifted = (ones_mask >> d)? If (ones >> d) has bit i = bit_{i+d}. Then positive count = popcount( ((ones >> d) & ~(ones <<?)) ) restricted n-d bits. Specifically mask_valid = (1 << (n-d)) - 1. positive = popcount( (ones >> d) & ~ones & mask_valid ). Since ~ones infinite bits, mask. Or ( (ones >> d) & (mask_valid ^ (ones & mask_valid)) ). Let's benchmark mentally: Python:
```
zero = full ^ mask
for d in range(1,n):
    valid = (1 << (n-d)) - 1
    c1 = ((mask >> d) & zero & valid).bit_count()
    c2 = (mask & (zero >> d) & valid).bit_count()
    if c1>max: max=c1
    if c2>max: max=c2
```
Creating valid mask each d with shift costly. Precompute valid_masks list of n-1 ints. Precompute? memory 512 ints fine. Then loop with local variables. Also (zero >> d) & valid. Let's design Markov initializer more deliberately to get high initial quality.
```
def markov_mask(n, p_stay, rng, target=None):
    state = rng.randint(2)
    bits = np.empty(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    bits[0]=state
    for i in range(1,n):
       if rng.rand() < p_stay: bits[i]=bits[i-1]
       else: bits[i]=1-bits[i-1]
    # balance
    ones = np.count_nonzero(bits)
    target = n//2
    if ones > target: flip random ones to 0
    elif ones < target: flip random zeros to 1
    return int mask
```
For n=512, generating 512*many in Python loop. Could generate using rng intervals to speed: geometric run lengths. Simpler Python loop 512 per init; 100 inits 51k steps fine.
But Markov loop with rng.rand each step maybe okay. Could use `rng.rand(n) < p_stay`? Need sequential; not vectorized easily but loop fine.

Better generate run lengths from Geometric distribution to control mean: `run_len = rng.geometric(1-q)`, with q=1-p_stay. Geometric gives number of trials until success? In numpy, `rng.geometric(p)` number of Bernoulli trials to get first success, mean 1/p. If success=switch, run length including current state = geometric(q) maybe mean 1/q. Sum until >n. Alternating states. This produces exact run lengths and may be faster.
```
def markov_mask_runs(n, q, rng, start_bit):
  mask=0 bits int? We can set ranges in Python int: pos=0; bit=start_bit
  while pos<n:
     l = rng.geometric(q) # maybe cap
     end=min(pos+l,n)
     if bit: mask |= ((1<<(end-pos))-1)<<pos
     pos=end; bit^=1
  # balance by flipping random bits of majority
```

Alternative: Use projected gradient of smooth max with efficient updates. h float, C float. Smooth objective S_β(h) = (1/β) log ∑ exp(β C_l) (where C_l = sum) may be differentiable. Gradient: For each coordinate a, derivative of S = (1/B) ∑_l exp(β C_l) ∂C_l/∂h_a. We can maintain weights w_l = softmax(β C_l) (sum=1). Then gradient for coordinate a = ∑_l w_l g_{a,l}, where g_{a,l} = derivative of C_l wrt h_a. This is similar to weighted sum objective W=∑w_l C_l; gradient is weighted sum of linear coefficients. We can perform projected gradient descent on W re-weighting periodically. Since W is bilinear, coordinate descent may be effective.

For fixed weights w, W=∑w_l C_l. We can minimize W via local pair moves (continuous)? For binary, swap moves changed integer. For continuous, we can choose pair (i,j) and adjust h_i down by δ, h_j up by δ (or vice versa) to maintain sum, with bounds. For fixed pair and fixed others, W as function of δ is linear! Because h_i and h_j changes linearly. So optimal at bounds (0/1) for that pair if weights fixed. Thus continuous minima for fixed weights at extremes, but when weights updated, a fractional point can balance. Iterative reweighting can produce fractional if active lags have opposing slopes for a coordinate.

This continuous targeted local search may be powerful and efficient. Let's formalize update and evaluation.

For positive d: C[pidx] = ∑_{i=0}^{n-d-1} h[i+d]*(1-h[i]). A contribution q_i = h[i+d]*(1-h[i]). Move ε from j=i+d to i: h_i_new=h_i+ε, h_j_new=h_j-ε. Its contribution changes from h_j(1-h_i) to (h_j-ε)(1-h_i-ε) = old - ε(1-h_i) - ε h_j + ε^2. For small ε, reduces by ε(1-h_i+h_j). Other lags affected via all k. We choose i where contribution high or where reducing helps.

For negative lag dd: C[nidx]=∑ h[i]*(1-h[i+dd]). Move ε from i to j=i+dd: contribution old h_i(1-h_j) -> (h_i-ε)(1-h_j+ε)=old -ε(1-h_j) -ε h_i +ε^2. Good.

Implement general apply_delta:
```
def apply_delta(h, C, p, delta, idx_cache):
    M = len(C)//2
    old = h[p]
    if p > 0:
        left = h[:p]
        idxp = idx_cache['left_pos'][p]
        idxn = idx_cache['left_neg'][p]
        C[idxp] += delta * (1.0 - left)
        C[idxn] -= delta * left
    if p + 1 < len(h):
        right = h[p+1:]
        idxp = idx_cache['right_pos'][p]
        idxn = idx_cache['right_neg'][p]
        C[idxp] -= delta * right
        C[idxn] += delta * (1.0 - right)
    new = old + delta
    C[M] += new*(1.0-new) - old*(1.0-old)
    h[p] = new
```
For binary with delta ±1, works. The C[M] update for binary old=0 delta=1 new1: 0; old=1 delta=-1 new0:0. Good.
Need h float64 for continuous. For binary local search, we can use int8 for speed but apply_delta generic float slower. We can separate: binary local search uses int8 h and C int32 with specialized apply_flip (faster). Continuous uses float64/float32. But we can reuse apply_delta with float64; fine for n=512 fewer moves. For binary local search, use integer arrays for speed. We can convert binary mask to h_int8/C_int32.

Index cache for n:
```
_idx_cache = {}
def build_idx_cache(n):
   if n in _idx_cache: return _idx_cache[n]
   M=n-1
   left_pos=[None]*n; left_neg=[None]*n; right_pos=[None]*n; right_neg=[None]*n
   for p in range(n):
      if p>0:
         d = p - np.arange(p) # [p,...,1]
         left_pos[p]=M+d
         left_neg[p]=M-d
      if p+1<n:
         d = np.arange(1, n-p)
         right_pos[p]=M+d
         right_neg[p]=M-d
   _idx_cache[n]=({'M':M,...arrays})
   return _idx_cache[n]
```
Storing arrays total O(n^2) per n: for n=1024, ~1M ints, 8MB each arrays*4=32MB, okay. But multiple n maybe. Could compute on fly to save memory; precompute speeds. For n=512 ~131k per side, fine. We'll build once per n.

Recompute C int/float:
```
def recompute_C(h, dtype=None):
    n=len(h); M=n-1
    C = np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=dtype)
    C[M] = np.sum(h*(1-h)) # if binary 0
    inv1_h = 1.0 - h if float? For int, 1-h
    for d in range(1,n):
       C[M+d] = np.dot(h[d:], 1-h[:n-d])
       C[M-d] = np.dot(h[:n-d], 1-h[d:])
    return C
```
For int8, `1 - h` int8; dot with int8 returns int64 maybe; assign to int32. Could use `np.dot`. For speed, maybe use matrix? Loop okay.
For float, dtype float64. To avoid computing 1-h each loop, precompute `one_minus = 1-h`? For h float changes not recompute? At recompute, yes. Can do:
```
omh = 1.0 - h
C[M+d] = np.dot(h[d:], omh[:n-d])
C[M-d] = np.dot(h[:n-d], omh[d:])
```
Yes.

`apply_flip` top-level:
```
def apply_flip(h,C,p,to_one,idx):
   M = idx['M']; n=len(h)
   if p>0:
      left = h[:p]
      idxp=idx['left_pos'][p]; idxn=idx['left_neg'][p]
      if to_one:
         C[idxp] += (1-left)
         C[idxn] -= left
      else:
         C[idxp] -= (1-left)
         C[idxn] += left
   if p+1<n:
      right = h[p+1:]
      idxp=idx['right_pos'][p]; idxn=idx['right_neg'][p]
      if to_one:
         C[idxp] -= right
         C[idxn] += (1-right)
      else:
         C[idxp] += right
         C[idxn] -= (1-right)
   h[p] = 1 if to_one else 0
```
Check C[M] no change. Use int8 left; `1-left` int8; C int32 += int8 array works? In-place add with casting 'same_kind'? int8 to int32 safe, should. But `C[idxp] += (1-left)` where RHS length p, LHS int32, dtype int8 -> NumPy ufunc add casting 'same_kind'? default 'same_kind' allows int64? int8 to int32 is safe kind, yes.
Potential overflow? C up to n=1024 int32 safe.

Acceptance details for binary:
```
def try_swap(h,C,i,j,cur_max,cur_num, idx, rng, accept_equal_prob=0.05, accept_worse_prob=0.0):
   hi= h[i]; hj=h[j]
   if hi==hj: return None
   to_i = (hi==0)
   to_j = (hj==0)
   apply_flip(h,C,i,to_i,idx)
   apply_flip(h,C,j,to_j,idx)
   max_idx = int(C.argmax())
   new_max = int(C[max_idx])
   accept=False
   if new_max < cur_max:
      accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C==new_max))
   elif new_max == cur_max:
      new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C==cur_max))
      if new_num < cur_num: accept=True
      elif new_num == cur_num and rng.rand() < accept_equal_prob: accept=True
      else: accept=False
   else:
      # maybe accept worse tiny
      if new_max <= cur_max + 1 and rng.rand() < accept_worse_prob:
         accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C==new_max))
   if accept:
      # update lists? do outside? We can return info.
      ...
      return ('accept', new_max, new_num, max_idx)
   else:
      apply_flip(h,C,j,not to_j? Wait to_j was new value for j. To revert, flip j to not to_j (old), then i to not to_i.
      apply_flip(h,C,j, (hj==1)) # old? If hj old=0, to_j=True, revert False; if hj old=1,to_j=False,revert True. old_to_j = (hj==1)? Since old value hj=1 if old to_one True. `old_to_i = (hi==1)`, `old_to_j=(hj==1)`. Revert j to old: to_one=old_to_j; i to old: old_to_i.
      return ('reject',)
```

def continuous_search(h_in, n, rng, timeout, eps_start=0.1):
    h = h_in.astype(float).copy()
    h = make_feasible_continuous(h,n) # clip, adjust sum to target
    C = recompute_C(h.astype(np.float64), dtype=float)
    cur_max = float(C.max())
    best_h=h.copy(); best_max=cur_max
    idx=build_idx_cache(n)
    nvar=n
    eps = eps_start
    no_improve=0; start=time.time()
    # optional top lag list dynamic
    while time...:
       max_idx=int(C.argmax()); cur_max=float(C[max_idx]); d=max_idx-M
       # choose move
       if d>0:
          contrib = h[d:] * (1 - h[:n-d])
          # candidate positions where contrib > small
          pos = np.where(contrib > 1e-6)[0]
          if len(pos)==0: break? choose random
          # choose random among top? For effectiveness choose random weighted by contrib? Simple random among pos; or pick max contrib.
          # Picking max contrib may reduce current max directly but may harm others. Random among top few.
          k = pos[rng.randint(len(pos))]
          source = k+d; target = k
       elif d<0:
          dd=-d
          contrib = h[:n-dd] * (1 - h[dd:])
          pos = np.where(contrib >1e-6)[0]
          if len(pos)>0:
             k = pos[rng.randint(len(pos))]
             source = k; target = k+dd
       else: random
       if random probability: source = random index with h>eps; target random with h<1-eps; choose such that h[source]-h[target] maybe? Direction source->target.
       max_eps = min(eps, h[source], 1-h[target])
       if max_eps <1e-8: continue
       # Optionally adjust eps schedule: start 0.2, reduce by 0.5 every 1000 no improve.
       apply_delta(h,C,source,-max_eps,idx)
       apply_delta(h,C,target,+max_eps,idx)
       new_max_idx=int(C.argmax()); new_max=float(C[new_max_idx])
       # secondary: sum top? If new_max<cur_max accept; if equal and maybe C sum near max lower.
       # For continuous, max real; accept if new_max < cur_max - 1e-10 or (abs<1e-10 and new_top_sum < old_top_sum)
       accept = new_max < cur_max - 1e-10
       if not accept and new_max == cur_max (within): compute penalty = np.sum(C[C > cur_max*0.95?]) maybe O(n). But continuous not binary; keep simple: if new_max <= cur_max + 1e-8 and rng.rand<0.1 accept? Could plateau.
       if accept:
          cur_max=new_max; update best; no_improve=0
          # maybe reduce eps slowly if many accepts? keep.
       else:
          apply_delta(h,C,source,+max_eps,idx); apply_delta(h,C,target,-max_eps,idx)
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>...: eps *=0.8, no_improve=0; maybe recompute C/h to remove drift
```

```
def markov_h_runs(n, r, rng):
   h=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.uint8); pos=0; state=rng.randint(2)
   while pos<n:
      l = rng.geometric(1-r)  # >=1
      end=min(pos+l,n)
      h[pos:end]=state
      pos=end
      state ^=1
```
This uses geometric distribution with mean 1/q, matches Markov. Faster. But `rng.geometric` Python random per run, not per bit, much faster. ```
def make_feasible_float(h,n):
   h=np.clip(h,0,1).astype(float)
   target=n/2.0
   diff=target-h.sum()
   for _ in range(100):
      if abs(diff)<1e-12: break
      if diff>0:
         mask=h<1-1e-12
         if mask.sum()==0: break
         h[mask]+=diff/mask.sum()
      else: ...
   # final adjust one index to exact target?
   diff=target-h.sum()
   # find index with room
   if abs(diff)>0:
      if diff>0: idx=np.argmax(1-h); h[idx]+=diff; clip
      else: idx=np.argmin(h); h[idx]+=diff
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   return h
```

Let's design a generic `optimize_n(n, init_list, time_budget, continuous_budget)` that:
- if init_list empty build.
- First evaluate all inits (recompute C), keep best.
- Then while time remaining > continuous_budget? and inits not exhausted/runs:
   For each init in inits (maybe shuffle) run binary_local_search with a segment time. But if local search from each init converges to same local, maybe waste. Limit segments. Use best init as seed for further local search from best.
   After local, run continuous_search from best binary and from parent interp if time.
- Return best (float h, C5, n).

We can integrate binary and continuous as one `search_candidate` that starts binary local search, then continuous. For each init, after binary local search (time seg), do continuous min time? But continuous from many inits costly. Use only best after all binary.

Pseudo:
```
def process_n(n, inits, time_budget, rng):
   # evaluate inits
   best_h=None; best_max=inf
   for h0 in inits:
      C0=recompute(C); c=c5
      if c<best: best...
   idx=build cache
   # binary search over inits
   t_start=time.time()
   i=0
   while time_left > 60:
      if i==0: h_cur=best_h? else h_cur=inits[i%len]
      seg = min(120, (time_budget - (time.time()-t_start))? Actually total for binary phase.
      h_s,C_s,max_s = binary_local_search(h_cur, n, rng, timeout=seg, best_limit=best_max?)
      if max_s<best_max: best...
      i+=1
   # continuous polish best
   if time_left > 30:
      h_cont, C_cont, max_cont = continuous_search(best_h, n, rng, timeout=remaining-10)
      if max_cont*2/n < best_c5: best...
   return best_h, c5
```

Pseudo for `binary_local_search`:
```
def binary_local_search(h_init, n, rng, timeout, idx=None):
    h = balance_binary(np.array(h_init,dtype=np.uint8).copy(), n, rng).astype(np.int8)
    C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.int32)
    max_idx = int(C.argmax()); cur_max=int(C[max_idx]); cur_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C==cur_max))
    best_h=h.copy(); best_max=cur_max; best_num=cur_num; best_C=C.copy()
    ones = np.where(h==1)[0].tolist(); zeros = np.where(h==0)[0].tolist()
    no_improve=0; start=time.time(); stall_limit=5000
    idx_m=idx if idx else build_idx_cache(n)
    M=idx_m['M']
    while time.time() - start < timeout and (no_improve < stall_limit or ...):
       if not ones or not zeros: break
       # choose move
       if rng.random_sample() < 0.35:
          max_idx_local=int(C.argmax()) # maybe use maintained? C.argmax each trial anyway
          d=max_idx_local - M
          if d>0:
             cand=np.where((h[:n-d]==0) & (h[d:]==1))[0]
             if len(cand): i=cand[rng.randint(len(cand))]; j=i+d
             else: choose random
          elif d<0: ...
          else random
       else random
       if i==j or h[i]==h[j]: continue
       # test
       accepted, new_max, new_num, new_idx, hi_old, hj_old = try_swap(...)
       if accepted:
          # update lists
          if hi_old==1:
             ones.remove(i); zeros.append(i); zeros.remove(j); ones.append(j)
          else:
             zeros.remove(i); ones.append(i); ones.remove(j); zeros.append(j)
          if new_max < best_max or (new_max==best_max and new_num<best_num):
             best_max=new_max; best_num=new_num; best_h=h.copy(); best_C=C.copy()
          cur_max=new_max; cur_num=new_num; cur_idx=new_idx
          no_improve=0
          # if best_max below lower theoretical? not know.
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > stall_limit:
             # maybe random perturbation: perform 10 random swaps accepted? Or break to next init
             # We'll do a modest random walk for 500 moves with equal accept higher
             ... or break
    return best_h, best_C, best_max
```
Stall handling: If no improvement (max or num) for stall_limit moves, break. The outer process can restart from another init or from best with different seed. But if current is best, break may waste. We can within same function after stall do a "restart" by taking current best and doing random perturbations (some random swaps even if worse) then search. Maybe easier: outer process calls multiple inits and also repeats from best.
Set stall_limit based on n: maybe 10000 for n=512, 20000 for n=1024. If break, return local best.
If timeout segment small, okay.

Important: `try_swap` updates C and h; if accept, C is final. If reject, C restored. But `C.argmax` in main before move may be outdated if C? We maintain cur idx but after rejects C same. Use maintained or compute each targeted.

`try_swap` implementation top-level with double apply:
```
def try_swap_binary(h,C,i,j,cur_max,cur_num,idx,rng,accept_equal_prob=0.05,accept_worse_prob=0.0005):
    hi = int(h[i]); hj=int(h[j])
    if hi == hj: return False, ...
    hi_to_one = (hi == 0); hj_to_one = (hj == 0)
    apply_flip(h,C,i,hi_to_one,idx)
    apply_flip(h,C,j,hj_to_one,idx)
    max_idx = int(C.argmax())
    new_max = int(C[max_idx])
    accept=False
    if new_max < cur_max:
       accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))
    elif new_max == cur_max:
       new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == cur_max))
       if new_num < cur_num:
          accept=True
       elif new_num == cur_num and rng.random_sample() < accept_equal_prob:
          accept=True
       else:
          accept=False
    else:
       if new_max <= cur_max + 1 and rng.random_sample() < accept_worse_prob:
          accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))
       else: accept=False
    if accept:
       return True, new_max, new_num, max_idx, hi, hj
    else:
       apply_flip(h,C,j,(hj==1),idx)
       apply_flip(h,C,i,(hi==1),idx)
       return False, 0,0,0,hi,hj
```
If accept worse, cur_max increases. Best_h tracked separately; cur after worse may not be best. That's okay. But if accept worse, the current state not best; no_improve? It is not improvement; should no_improve increase. In main, if new_max > cur_max, even accepted, no_improve maybe not reset. We'll treat only max decrease or num decrease as progress. If worse accepted, no_improve increases. Acceptance probability very low.
For equal accepted via random prob, cur_num same, no progress; no_improve increases.
For max decrease, progress; num decrease progress.
List update uses hi/hj old.
Need `np.count_nonzero(C == new_max)` on int32, okay.

Potential issue: `C.argmax` returns first max. For num max count, if new_max<cur, count at new_max may be high. Good.

`targeted_swap_candidates`:
```
def targeted_swap(h,M,max_idx,n,rng):
   d=max_idx-M
   if d==0: return None
   if d>0:
      cand = np.where((h[:n-d] == 0) & (h[d:] == 1))[0]
   else:
      dd=-d
      cand = np.where((h[:n-dd] == 1) & (h[dd:] == 0))[0]
   if len(cand)==0: return None
   k = cand[rng.randint(len(cand))]
   return k, k+abs(d)
```
Maybe choose not random but candidate that produces best move? Could evaluate top few. Too expensive. Random among contributors.

Could choose i/j for targeted that also reduces num max? Random okay.

Random move from lists: Since after continuous? Binary only.
```
i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
```
This is O(1). But lists may get unsorted; fine.

List updates: `ones.remove(i)` can fail if duplicate? h binary no duplicates. O(n).

Performance: recomputing `np.where` for targeted each targeted trial O(n). 35% of trials. Fine.

Now continuous local search.
Need `apply_delta` uses idx arrays. For float h, C float. The update formulas are linear but if h values float, C[M] update as `delta*(1.0 - 2.0*old - delta)`. For pair source/target with eps, sum preserved.
```
def apply_delta(h,C,p,delta,idx):
    M=idx['M']; n=len(h)
    old=h[p]
    if p>0:
       left=h[:p]
       C[idx['left_pos'][p]] += delta*(1.0-left)
       C[idx['left_neg'][p]] -= delta*left
    if p+1<n:
       right=h[p+1:]
       C[idx['right_pos'][p]] -= delta*right
       C[idx['left?']] # careful right_neg
    C[M] += delta*(1.0 - 2.0*old - delta)
    new=old+delta
    h[p]=new
```
No clipping; caller ensures bounds. For performance, use float64; temps. n* moves maybe less. We can optimize by using local variables.
Need if delta is very small, C[M] change quadratic; okay.

Continuous search:
```
def continuous_local_search(h_init, n, rng, timeout, eps_start=0.2, idx=None):
   h = make_feasible_float(np.asarray(h_init,dtype=float).copy(), n)
   C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.float64)
   cur_max = float(C.max()); cur_score = top_score(C, 2.0)
   best_h=h.copy(); best_max=cur_max; best_score=cur_score
   idx=idx or build_idx_cache(n); M=idx['M']
   eps=eps_start; no_improve=0; start=time.time()
   stall_limit=2000
   while time...:
      max_idx=int(C.argmax()); cur_max=float(C[max_idx])
      # if cur_max < best_max? no, best tracked
      # choose move
      move=None
      r=rng.random_sample()
      d=max_idx-M
      if r < 0.8 and d != 0:
         if d > 0:
            contrib = h[d:] * (1.0 - h[:n-d])
            th = max(contrib.max()*0.5, 1e-4)? if max 0 skip
            pos = np.where(contrib > th)[0]
            if len(pos)>0:
               k = pos[rng.randint(len(pos))]
               src = k+d; tgt=k; move=(src,tgt)
         else:
            dd=-d
            contrib = h[:n-dd]*(1.0-h[dd:])
            th=max(contrib.max()*0.5,1e-4)
            pos=np.where(contrib>th)[0]
            if len(pos)>0:
               k=pos[rng.randint(len(pos))]
               src=k; tgt=k+dd; move=(src,tgt)
      if move is None:
         # random high->low
         # choose source from h>1e-4, target from h<1-1e-4 multiple tries
         for _ in range(10):
            s=rng.randint(n); t=rng.randint(n)
            if s==t: continue
            if h[s] < 1e-4 or h[t] > 1-1e-4: continue
            if h[s] < h[t]: s,t=t,s
            move=(s,t); break
         if move is None: break
      src,tgt=move
      max_eps = min(eps, float(h[src]), 1.0 - float(h[tgt]))
      if max_eps < 1e-8: continue
      e=max_eps
      apply_delta(h,C,src,-e,idx)
      apply_delta(h,C,tgt,e,idx)
      new_max_idx=int(C.argmax()); new_max=float(C[new_max_idx])
      accept=False
      if new_max < cur_max - 1e-10:
         accept=True
      elif new_max <= cur_max + 1e-8:
         new_score=top_score(C, 2.0)
         if new_score < cur_score - 1e-10:
             accept=True
         elif abs(new_score-cur_score)<=1e-10 and rng.random_sample()<0.01:
             accept=True
      if accept:
         cur_score = new_score if new_score defined else top_score(C,2.0)
         if new_max < best_max - 1e-12:
             best_max=new_max; best_h=h.copy()
         elif new_max == best_max and ...
         no_improve=0
         # optionally increase eps if many accepts? no
      else:
         apply_delta(h,C,src,e,idx)
         apply_delta(h,C,tgt,-e,idx)
         no_improve +=1
         if no_improve > stall_limit:
             eps = max(0.005, eps*0.75); no_improve=0
             # recompute to clean
             h = make_feasible_float(h,n); C=recompute_C(h,float); cur_max=float(C.max()); cur_score=top_score(C,2.0); no_improve=0
      # Optional: if best_max <= target count? We don't know target? We can continue.
   return best_h, best_max
```
For top_score, using `max(0, C - (max(C)-tol))` but if we call after C modified and have new_max, avoid C.max:
```
def top_score_withmax(C, new_max, tol=2.0):
   thr = max(0.0, new_max - tol)
   return float(np.sum(C > thr) * (C > thr)) # C > thr creates bool; multiply
```
Use `np.maximum(C - thr, 0.0).sum()`.
When accept equal and new_score computed; if accept by max decrease, we need cur_score for next. We can compute `cur_score = top_score_withmax(C, new_max, 2.0)` after any acceptance (or recompute). O(n) fine.
For progress: if new_max < best_max update best. If equal and new_score < best_score maybe update? Objective only max, but best_h for continuous maybe not needed? We can track best max only. However if current max equal but top score lower, not necessary for objective; but can enable further. We don't need best_score for final. But can update best_h if same max and lower score? Not needed; final only max. But if later current worsens, best saved. We'll save if new_max < best_max (or tie maybe any).

Continuous from binary with h int8: `make_feasible_float` preserves sum? For binary sum target; okay.

Can continuous move to fractional and then apply binary local search on rounded continuous? Maybe after some continuous, we can round and binary search again to exploit. Could help escape binary local optima? We can include if time.

But rounding a continuous optimum may reduce fractional gains; only keep if lower.

`create_inits(n, rng, h_parent)`:
```
inits=[]
if h_parent is not None:
   if len(h_parent) > 0:
      x_old=np.linspace(0,2,len(h_parent),endpoint=False)
      x_new=np.linspace(0,2,n,endpoint=False)
      hp=np.interp(x_new,x_old,h_parent)
      # threshold at 0.5
      hb=(hp>=0.5).astype(np.uint8); inits.append(balance_binary(hb,n,rng))
      # threshold at 0.45? Balance; also maybe sample quantile to ensure n/2: sort values, top half.

`markov_h` using rng.geometric: Need ensure no infinite loop if q tiny? r<=0.999, q>=0.001, geometric mean1000 but n max maybe 2048; okay. Use `max_len = 5*n` cap? If geometric > n, set n.
```
def markov_h(n, r, rng):
    h=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    if n==0: return h
    q = max(1e-6, 1.0 - r)
    state = rng.randint(2)
    pos=0
    while pos < n:
       l = int(rng.geometric(q))
       if l > n - pos: l = n - pos
       h[pos:pos+l] = state
       pos += l
       state = 1 - state
    return h
```
This yields run lengths geometric. Balance flips random.

Could improve Markov balance by adjusting a run length instead of random flips. The expected count from Markov without conditioning is n/2; deviation small. Random flips fine. def balance_binary(h,n,rng):
   target=n//2
   c=int(h.sum())
   if c>target:
      idx=np.where(h==1)[0]
      flip=rng.choice(idx, c-target, replace=False); h[flip]=0
   elif c<target: ...
   return h
```

Balanced continuous feasibility:
```
def make_feasible_float(h,n):
   h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).copy()
   h = np.clip(h,0,1)
   target = n/2.0
   for _ in range(1000):
      s=np.sum(h); diff=target-s
      if abs(diff) <= 1e-12: break
      if diff > 0:
         mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-12
         if not mask.any(): break
         inc = diff / mask.sum()
         if inc > 1e-12:
            h[mask] += inc
         else:
            # add to one element with room
            idxs=np.flatnonzero(mask)
            if idxs.size==0: break
            add = min(diff, (1-h[idxs[0]]) if diff>0 else 0)
            h[idxs[0]] += add
      else:
         mask = h > 1e-12
         if not mask.any(): break
         dec = diff / mask.sum()
         if dec < -1e-12:
            h[mask] += dec
         else:
            idxs=np.flatnonzero(mask)
            if idxs.size==0: break
            add = max(diff, -h[idxs[0]])
            h[idxs[0]] += add
      h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   # final exact adjust
   diff=target-np.sum(h)
   if diff !=0:
      if diff>0:
         idxs=np.flatnonzero(h < 1.0)
         if idxs.size:
            add = min(diff, 1.0 - h[idxs[0]])
            h[idxs[0]] += add
      else:
         idxs=np.flatnonzero(h > 0.0)
         if idxs.size:
            add = max(diff, -h[idxs[0]])
            h[idxs[0]] += add
   return np.clip(h,0,1)
```
If final exact impossible due floating precision, okay.

`balance_binary` for n even always. If n odd? We choose even n. But robust: target=n//2? Constraint sum n/2 exactly; n must be even. We choose even.

```
def process_n(n, inits, rng, time_limit, idx=None):
    # evaluate inits early
    best_h=None; best_max=inf
    for h0 in inits:
       h0=balance_binary(h0,n,rng)
       C0=recompute_C(h0,dtype=np.int32)
       m=int(C0.max())
       if m<best_max: best_max=m; best_h=h0.copy()
    if best_h is None: return None, inf
    if time_limit <= 0: return best_h, best_max
    idx = idx or build_idx_cache(n)
    total_start=time.time()
    # binary local search
    search_inits = [best_h] + [init for init in inits if init is not best_h?] Maybe iterate.
    # To avoid lists memory, shuffle? Use rng shuffle on list of arrays? We'll just loop.
    i=0
    no_improve_global=0
    while time.time() - total_start < time_limit and (no_improve_global < 5 or time...):
       # choose seed: first best, then cyclic inits
       if i == 0:
          seed_h = best_h
          seg = min(120, max(20, time_limit*0.5))
       else:
          seed_h = inits[(i-1) % len(inits)]
          remaining = time_limit - (time.time() - total_start)
          # segment = min(90, max(15, remaining / max(1, len(inits)*2?)))
       if time.time() - total_start > time_limit: break
       rem = time_limit - (time.time() - total_start)
       if rem < 15: break
       seg = min(90.0, rem)
       # If i>0 and many inits, maybe seg= min(45, rem/len)
       if i > 0:
          seg = min(45.0, max(15.0, rem / max(1,len(inits))))
       h_s, C_s, max_s = binary_local_search(seed_h, n, rng, timeout=seg, idx=idx)
       if max_s < best_max:
          best_max = max_s; best_h = h_s.copy()
          no_improve_global=0
       else:
          no_improve_global += 1
       i += 1
       if no_improve_global > 4 and i > len(inits):
          # only keep best and do more from best with smaller segments
          no_improve_global=0
          inits = [best_h]
       # If best_max reaches minimum possible? Unknown. Could if c5 <= target? But continue for lower.
    # continuous polish within remaining
    cont_start=time.time()
    cont_budget = time_limit - (cont_start - total_start) - 5
    if cont_budget > 20:
       # continuous from best binary; also if parent interp? h from best.
       h_cont, max_cont = continuous_local_search(best_h, n, rng, timeout=cont_budget, idx=idx)
       # max_cont is count float
       c_cont = max_cont * (2.0/n)
       c_best = best_max * (2.0/n)
       if c_cont < c_best - 1e-12:
          best_h = h_cont; best_max = max_cont  # but best_h float, best_max count
    return best_h, best_max
```

def quick_scan_n(n, rng, h_parent, n_samples, time_limit):
   inits = []
   if h_parent: add top binary parent
   rlist = choose
   for r in rlist:
      for rep in range(2):
         hm=markov_h(n,r,rng)
         inits.append(balance_binary(hm,n,rng))
         if rep==0: inits.append(balance_binary(np.roll(hm, rng.randint(n)), np.uint8),n,rng))
   # possibly trim to n_samples
   best_h=None; best_c5=inf
   for h0 in inits:
      if time... break
      C=recompute_C(h0, dtype=np.int32)
      c=float(C.max())*2/n
      if c<best_c5: best=h0,c
   return best_h,best_c5

def choose_markov_r(n):

  if n <=512: return (0.95,0.96,0.97,0.98,0.985,0.99)

def create_inits(n,rng,h_parent):
  inits=[]
  if h_parent is not None:
     hp=interp_parent(h_parent,n)
     # top half
     if n>0:
        idx=np.argsort(hp)[-n//2:]
        hb=np.zeros(n,np.uint8); hb[idx]=1; inits.append(hb)
        hb=(hp>=0.5).astype(np.uint8); inits.append(balance_binary(hb,n,rng))
     else: ...
  rlist=choose...
  reps = 2 if n<=1024 else 1
  rolls = 1 if n<=1024 else 0
  for r in rlist:
      for rep in range(reps):
         hm=markov_h(n,r,rng)
         inits.append(balance_binary(hm,n,rng))
         if rolls>0:
            sh=rng.randint(n)
            inits.append(balance_binary(np.roll(hm,sh).astype(np.uint8),n,rng))
  # random
  if n<=1024:
      hr=(rng.rand(n)>0.5).astype(np.uint8); inits.append(balance_binary(hr,n,rng))
  # thue-morse maybe for n<=512
  if n<=512: inits.append(thue_morse_binary(n))
  return inits
```

`thue_morse_binary`:
```
def thue_morse_binary(n):
   h=np.zeros(n,np.uint8)
   for i in range(1,n):
      h[i] = h[i>>1] ^ (i & 1)
   return h # popcount parity; balance not necessarily
```

`process_n` returns h and c5 maybe. Let's make it compute c5 direct and return c5 to avoid confusion.
```
def process_n(n, inits, rng, time_limit):
   best_h=None; best_c5=inf
   # evaluate all inits
   for h0 in inits:
      hb=balance_binary(h0,n,rng)
      C0=recompute_C(hb,dtype=np.int32)
      c=float(C0.max())*(2.0/n)
      if c<best_c5: best_c5=c; best_h=hb.copy()
   if best_h is None: return None, inf
   if time_limit <= 1: return best_h,best_c5
   idx=build_idx_cache(n)
   total_start=time.time()
   # Binary local search
   i=0; no_global=0
   # We'll first search from best, then inits, then best again.
   while time.time() - total_start < time_limit:
      rem = time_limit - (time.time() - total_start)
      if rem < 20: break
      if i == 0:
         seed = best_h
         seg = min(120.0, rem, time_limit*0.5)
      else:
         # if no global improve for too many, only best
         if no_global > 3:
            seed = best_h
            seg = min(60.0, rem)
         else:
            seed = inits[(i-1) % len(inits)] if (i-1) < len(inits)*2 else best_h
            seg = min(45.0, max(15.0, rem / 4.0))
      if seg <= 5: break
      h_s, max_count = binary_local_search(seed, n, rng, timeout=seg, idx=idx)
      c_s = float(max_count)*(2.0/n)
      if c_s < best_c5 - 1e-12:
          best_c5 = c_s; best_h = h_s.copy(); no_global=0
      else:
          no_global += 1
      i += 1
      if no_global > 8 and i > 10:
          break
   # continuous if time remains
   cont_start=time.time()
   cont_rem = time_limit - (cont_start - total_start) - 5
   if cont_rem > 20:
       h_cont, max_cont = continuous_local_search(best_h, n, rng, timeout=cont_rem, idx=idx)
       c_cont = float(max_cont)*(2.0/n)
       if c_cont < best_c5 - 1e-12:
           best_c5 = c_cont; best_h = h_cont
   # recompute final c5 to ensure actual
   # If best_h float or int, make feasible float? For binary no.
   h_final = make_feasible_float(best_h, n) if best_h.dtype != np.uint8 and best_h.dtype != np.int8 else best_h.astype(np.float64)
   C_final = recompute_C(h_final, dtype=np.float64)
   best_c5 = float(C_final.max())*(2.0/n)
   return h_final, best_c5
```
But if best_h is int8, `make_feasible_float` will clip and adjust sum; binary sum exact. It returns float. Good.

`recompute_C`:
```
def recompute_C(h, dtype=None):
    h = np.asarray(h)
    n = len(h)
    if dtype is None:
       dtype = h.dtype if h.dtype != np.uint8 else np.int32
    C = np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=dtype)
    if n == 0: return C
    omh = 1.0 - h if np.issubdtype(h.dtype, np.floating) else (1 - h)
    # For int h, omh int8; dot int8*int8 -> int64? Assign to int32.
    C[n-1] = np.sum(h * omh, dtype=dtype?) 
```

`apply_flip`: For C int and h int. It doesn't check dtype. For to_one bool.
Potential issue: If `left` int8, `1-left` creates uint? `1` int -> int64? Fine. We can cast? Not.
```
def apply_flip(h, C, p, to_one, idx):
    M=idx['M']; n=len(h)
    if p>0:
       left=h[:p]
       idxp=idx['left_pos'][p]; idxn=idx['left_neg'][p]
       if to_one:
          C[idxp] += (1 - left)
          C[idxn] -= left
       else:
          C[idxp] -= (1 - left)
          C[idxn] += left
    if p+1<n:
       right=h[p+1:]
       idxp=idx['right_pos'][p]; idxn=idx['right_neg'][p]
       if to_one:
          C[idxp] -= right
          C[idxn] += (1 - right)
       else:
          C[idxp] += right
          C[idxn] -= (1 - right)
    h[p] = 1 if to_one else 0
```
For h uint8, assignment 1 works. For int8. In binary search h int8. Good.

`try_swap_binary`:
```
def try_swap_binary(h,C,i,j,cur_max,cur_num,idx,rng,accept_equal_prob=0.05,accept_worse_prob=0.0005):
   hi=int(h[i]); hj=int(h[j])
   if hi == hj: return (False,0,0,0,hi,hj)
   apply_flip(h,C,i,(hi==0),idx)
   apply_flip(h,C,j,(hj==0),idx)
   max_idx=int(C.argmax())
   new_max=int(C[max_idx])
   accept=False; new_num=0
   if new_max < cur_max:
      accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))
   elif new_max == cur_max:
      new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == cur_max))
      if new_num < cur_num:
         accept=True
      elif new_num == cur_num and rng.random_sample() < accept_equal_prob:
         accept=True
   else:
      if new_max <= cur_max + 1 and rng.random_sample() < accept_worse_prob:
         accept=True; new_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))
   if not accept:
      apply_flip(h,C,j,(hj==1),idx)
      apply_flip(h,C,i,(hi==1),idx)
      return (False,0,0,0,hi,hj)
   return (True,new_max,new_num,max_idx,hi,hj)
```
If new_max > cur_max+1 accept false. If accept worse, cur_num? returned. In main, if accepted, update lists. We need know if progress:
```
progress = (new_max < cur_max) or (new_max == cur_max and new_num < cur_num)
if accepted:
  update lists
  if c_s? best local...
  cur_max=new_max; cur_num=new_num; cur_idx=new_max_idx
  if progress: no_improve=0
  else: no_improve+=1
```
But in `try_swap`, for equal accepted via random, progress false. For max decrease true. For num decrease true. For worse accepted false. We can compute in main.

def binary_local_search(h_init, n, rng, timeout, idx=None):
    h = balance_binary(np.asarray(h_init, dtype=np.uint8).copy(), n, rng).astype(np.int8)
    C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.int32)
    cur_idx=int(C.argmax()); cur_max=int(C[cur_idx]); cur_num=int(np.count_nonzero(C==cur_max))
    best_h=h.copy(); best_max=cur_max; best_num=cur_num
    ones = [int(x) for x in np.flatnonzero(h==1)]
    zeros = [int(x) for x in np.flatnonzero(h==0)]

    M=idx['M']

```
def top_score(C, max_val, tol=2.0):
   thr = max(0.0, float(max_val) - tol)
   return float(np.sum(np.maximum(C - thr, 0.0)))
```

In `balance_binary`, using `rng.choice` returns array. If excess 0, no call. If idx size equal excess, choice okay replace=False. For large arrays, fine.
```
def balance_binary(h,n,rng):
   h=np.asarray(h,dtype=np.uint8).copy()
   target=n//2
   c=int(h.sum())
   if c>target:
      idx=np.flatnonzero(h==1)
      if idx.size:
         take=rng.choice(idx, c-target, replace=False)
         h[take]=0
   elif c<target:
      idx=np.flatnonzero(h==0)
      if idx.size:
         take=rng.choice(idx, target-c, replace=False)
         h[take]=1
   return h
```
If n odd? target floor, but constraint n/2 integer; we use even. If c-target > idx.size, choice fails; but c is idx.size, so no.

`markov_h`:
```
def markov_h(n,r,rng):
    h=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.uint8)
    if n<=0: return h
    q=1.0-r
    if q <=0: q=1e-6
    if q >=1: q=1.0
    state=rng.randint(2)
    pos=0
    while pos<n:
       l=int(rng.geometric(q))
       rem=n-pos
       if l>rem: l=rem
       h[pos:pos+l]=state
       pos+=l
       state=1-state
    return h
```
`rng.geometric(q)` requires q in (0,1]. If q very small 0.002 okay. If r=0.998 q=0.002. Good.

`interp_parent`:
```
def interp_parent(hp,n):
   if len(hp) < 1: return np.full(n,0.5)
   x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,len(hp),endpoint=False)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n,endpoint=False)
   return np.interp(x_new,x_old,hp)
```

def c5_by_correlate(h):
   n=len(h)
   if n==0: return 0.0
   corr=np.correlate(h.astype(np.float64), 1.0-h.astype(np.float64), mode='full')
   return float(np.max(corr) * (2.0/n))

def finalize_solution(h, n):
    if h is None: return None, float('inf')
    h_arr = np.asarray(h)
    if h_arr.size == 0: return h_arr.astype(float), 0.0
    if np.issubdtype(h_arr.dtype, np.integer):
       h_out = h_arr.astype(np.float64)
    else:
       h_out = make_feasible_float(h_arr.astype(np.float64).copy(), n)
    c = c5_by_correlate(h_out)
    return h_out, float(c)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

_IDX_CACHE = {}


def build_idx_cache(n):
    if n in _IDX_CACHE:
        return _IDX_CACHE[n]
    M = n - 1
    left_pos = [None] * n
    left_neg = [None] * n
    right_pos = [None] * n
    right_neg = [None] * n
    for p in range(n):
        if p > 0:
            d = p - np.arange(p, dtype=np.int32)
            left_pos[p] = M + d
            left_neg[p] = M - d
        if p + 1 < n:
            d = np.arange(1, n - p, dtype=np.int32)
            right_pos[p] = M + d
            right_neg[p] = M - d
    out = {
        "M": M,
        "left_pos": left_pos,
        "left_neg": left_neg,
        "right_pos": right_pos,
        "right_neg": right_neg,
    }
    _IDX_CACHE[n] = out
    return out


def recompute_C(h, dtype=None):
    arr = np.asarray(h)
    n = len(arr)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=float if dtype is None else dtype)
    if dtype is None:
        dtype = np.float64
    C = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=dtype)
    M = n - 1

    if np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.floating):
        a = arr.astype(np.float64, copy=False)
        om = 1.0 - a
        if np.issubdtype(C.dtype, np.integer):
            C[M] = int(np.dot(a, om))
            for d in range(1, n):
                C[M + d] = int(np.dot(a[d:], om[:n - d]))
                C[M - d] = int(np.dot(a[:n - d], om[d:]))
        else:
            C[M] = float(np.dot(a, om))
            for d in range(1, n):
                C[M + d] = float(np.dot(a[d:], om[:n - d]))
                C[M - d] = float(np.dot(a[:n - d], om[d:]))
    else:
        a = arr.astype(np.int8, copy=False)
        om = 1 - a
        if np.issubdtype(C.dtype, np.integer):
            C[M] = int(np.dot(a, om))
            for d in range(1, n):
                C[M + d] = int(np.dot(a[d:], om[:n - d]))
                C[M - d] = int(np.dot(a[:n - d], om[d:]))
        else:
            af = a.astype(np.float64)
            omf = (1.0 - af)
            C[M] = float(np.dot(af, omf))
            for d in range(1, n):
                C[M + d] = float(np.dot(af[d:], omf[:n - d]))
                C[M - d] = float(np.dot(af[:n - d], omf[d:]))
    return C


def make_feasible_float(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if len(h) != n:
        h = np.resize(h, n)
    h[np.isnan(h)] = 0.5
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = n / 2.0

    for _ in range(1000):
        s = float(np.sum(h))
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            mask = h < 1.0 - 1e-12
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
            if cnt == 0:
                break
            inc = diff / cnt
            if inc > 1e-12:
                h[mask] += inc
            else:
                idx = int(np.flatnonzero(mask)[0])
                h[idx] += min(diff, 1.0 - h[idx])
        else:
            mask = h > 1e-12
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
            if cnt == 0:
                break
            dec = diff / cnt
            if dec < -1e-12:
                h[mask] += dec
            else:
                idx = int(np.flatnonzero(mask)[0])
                h[idx] += max(diff, -h[idx])
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 0.0:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idxs = np.flatnonzero(h < 1.0)
            if idxs.size > 0:
                idx = int(idxs[0])
                h[idx] += min(diff, 1.0 - h[idx])
        else:
            idxs = np.flatnonzero(h > 0.0)
            if idxs.size > 0:
                idx = int(idxs[0])
                h[idx] += max(diff, -h[idx])
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def balance_binary(h, n, rng):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.uint8).copy()
    if len(h) != n:
        h = np.resize(h, n)
    target = n // 2
    c = int(np.sum(h))
    if c > target:
        idx = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
        if idx.size > 0:
            need = c - target
            if need >= idx.size:
                h[idx] = 0
            else:
                take = rng.choice(idx, need, replace=False)
                h[take] = 0
    elif c < target:
        idx = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
        if idx.size > 0:
            need = target - c
            if need >= idx.size:
                h[idx] = 1
            else:
                take = rng.choice(idx, need, replace=False)
                h[take] = 1
    return h


def markov_h(n, r, rng):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    if n <= 0:
        return h
    q = 1.0 - float(r)
    if q <= 0.0:
        q = 1e-6
    if q >= 1.0:
        q = 1.0
    state = int(rng.randint(2))
    pos = 0
    while pos < n:
        l = int(rng.geometric(q))
        rem = n - pos
        if l > rem:
            l = rem
        h[pos:pos + l] = state
        pos += l
        state = 1 - state
    return h


def interp_parent(hp, n):
    hp = np.asarray(hp, dtype=np.float64)
    if hp.size < 1:
        return np.full(n, 0.5)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, hp.size, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n, endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, hp)


def thue_morse_binary(n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
    if n <= 0:
        return h
    for i in range(1, n):
        h[i] = h[i >> 1] ^ (i & 1)
    return h


def choose_markov_r(n):
    if n <= 256:
        return (0.93, 0.94, 0.95, 0.96, 0.97, 0.98)
    if n <= 512:
        return (0.95, 0.96, 0.97, 0.98, 0.985, 0.99)
    if n <= 1024:
        return (0.985, 0.99, 0.992, 0.994, 0.995, 0.996)
    return (0.994, 0.9955, 0.9965, 0.9975, 0.9985)


def create_inits(n, rng, h_parent):
    inits = []
    if n <= 0:
        return inits

    if h_parent is not None and len(h_parent) > 0:
        hp = interp_parent(h_parent, n)
        top = np.argsort(hp)[- (n // 2):]
        hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.uint8)
        hb[top] = 1
        inits.append(hb)

        hb2 = (hp >= 0.5).astype(np.uint8)
        inits.append(balance_binary(hb2, n, rng))

    rlist = choose_markov_r(n)
    if n <= 1024:
        reps = 2
        rolls = 1
    else:
        reps = 1
        rolls = 0

    for r in rlist:
        for _rep in range(reps):
            hm = markov_h(n, r, rng)
            hm_bal = balance_binary(hm, n, rng)
            inits.append(hm_bal.copy())
            if rolls > 0:
                sh = int(rng.randint(n))
                hm_roll = np.roll(hm, sh)
                inits.append(balance_binary(hm_roll, n, rng))

    if n <= 1024:
        hr = (rng.rand(n) > 0.5).astype(np.uint8)
        inits.append(balance_binary(hr, n, rng))

    if n <= 512:
        inits.append(balance_binary(thue_morse_binary(n), n, rng))

    if len(inits) == 0:
        inits.append(balance_binary(thue_morse_binary(n), n, rng))
    return inits


def apply_flip(h, C, p, to_one, idx):
    M = idx["M"]
    n = len(h)
    if p > 0:
        left = h[:p]
        idxp = idx["left_pos"][p]
        idxn = idx["left_neg"][p]
        if to_one:
            C[idxp] += (1 - left)
            C[idxn] -= left
        else:
            C[idxp] -= (1 - left)
            C[idxn] += left
    if p + 1 < n:
        right = h[p + 1:]
        idxp = idx["right_pos"][p]
        idxn = idx["right_neg"][p]
        if to_one:
            C[idxp] -= right
            C[idxn] += (1 - right)
        else:
            C[idxp] += right
            C[idxn] -= (1 - right)
    h[p] = 1 if to_one else 0


def remove_value(lst, x):
    for pos, val in enumerate(lst):
        if val == x:
            lst.pop(pos)
            return


def try_swap_binary(h, C, i, j, cur_max, cur_num, idx, rng,
                    accept_equal_prob=0.02, accept_worse_prob=0.0):
    hi = int(h[i])
    hj = int(h[j])
    if hi == hj:
        return (False, 0, 0, 0, hi, hj)

    apply_flip(h, C, i, (hi == 0), idx)
    apply_flip(h, C, j, (hj == 0), idx)

    max_idx = int(C.argmax())
    new_max = int(C[max_idx])
    accept = False
    new_num = 0

    if new_max < cur_max:
        accept = True
        new_num = int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))
    elif new_max == cur_max:
        new_num = int(np.count_nonzero(C == cur_max))
        if new_num < cur_num:
            accept = True
        elif new_num == cur_num and rng.random_sample() < accept_equal_prob:
            accept = True
    else:
        if new_max <= cur_max + 1 and rng.random_sample() < accept_worse_prob:
            accept = True
            new_num = int(np.count_nonzero(C == new_max))

    if not accept:
        apply_flip(h, C, j, (hj == 1), idx)
        apply_flip(h, C, i, (hi == 1), idx)
        return (False, 0, 0, 0, hi, hj)

    return (True, new_max, new_num, max_idx, hi, hj)


def binary_local_search(h_init, n, rng, timeout, idx=None):
    arr = np.asarray(h_init)
    if np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.floating):
        h0 = (arr >= 0.5).astype(np.uint8)
    else:
        h0 = arr.astype(np.uint8)
    h = balance_binary(h0, n, rng).astype(np.int8)
    C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.int32)

    cur_idx = int(C.argmax())
    cur_max = int(C[cur_idx])
    cur_num = int(np.count_nonzero(C == cur_max))

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_max = cur_max
    best_num = cur_num

    ones = [int(x) for x in np.flatnonzero(h == 1)]
    zeros = [int(x) for x in np.flatnonzero(h == 0)]

    if idx is None:
        idx = build_idx_cache(n)
    M = idx["M"]

    start_t = time.time()
    no_improve = 0
    stall_limit = 12000 if n <= 512 else 7000
    trial = 0
    max_trials = 10000000

    while trial < max_trials and (time.time() - start_t) < timeout:
        trial += 1
        if len(ones) == 0 or len(zeros) == 0:
            break

        if no_improve >= stall_limit:
            for _pw in range(3):
                if len(ones) == 0 or len(zeros) == 0:
                    break
                i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
                j = zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
                hi = int(h[i])
                hj = int(h[j])
                if hi == hj:
                    continue
                apply_flip(h, C, i, (hi == 0), idx)
                apply_flip(h, C, j, (hj == 0), idx)
                if hi == 1:
                    remove_value(ones, i)
                    zeros.append(i)
                    remove_value(zeros, j)
                    ones.append(j)
                else:
                    remove_value(zeros, i)
                    ones.append(i)
                    remove_value(ones, j)
                    zeros.append(j)
            cur_idx = int(C.argmax())
            cur_max = int(C[cur_idx])
            cur_num = int(np.count_nonzero(C == cur_max))
            if cur_max < best_max or (cur_max == best_max and cur_num < best_num):
                best_max = cur_max
                best_num = cur_num
                best_h = h.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        i = None
        j = None
        if rng.random_sample() < 0.35:
            max_idx = cur_idx
            d = max_idx - M
            if d > 0:
                cand = np.where((h[:n - d] == 0) & (h[d:] == 1))[0]
                if len(cand) > 0:
                    k = int(cand[rng.randint(len(cand))])
                    i = k
                    j = k + d
            elif d < 0:
                dd = -d
                cand = np.where((h[:n - dd] == 1) & (h[dd:] == 0))[0]
                if len(cand) > 0:
                    k = int(cand[rng.randint(len(cand))])
                    i = k
                    j = k + dd

        if i is None or i == j or h[i] == h[j]:
            i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
            j = zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
            if i == j:
                continue

        old_max = cur_max
        old_num = cur_num
        accepted, new_max, new_num, new_idx, hi, hj = try_swap_binary(
            h, C, i, j, old_max, old_num, idx, rng,
            accept_equal_prob=0.02, accept_worse_prob=0.0
        )

        if accepted:
            if hi == 1:
                remove_value(ones, i)
                zeros.append(i)
                remove_value(zeros, j)
                ones.append(j)
            else:
                remove_value(zeros, i)
                ones.append(i)
                remove_value(ones, j)
                zeros.append(j)

            cur_max = new_max
            cur_num = new_num
            cur_idx = new_idx

            progress = (new_max < old_max) or (new_max == old_max and new_num < old_num)
            if progress:
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1

            if new_max < best_max or (new_max == best_max and new_num < best_num):
                best_max = new_max
                best_num = new_num
                best_h = h.copy()
        else:
            no_improve += 1

    C_best = recompute_C(best_h, dtype=np.int32)
    best_max = int(C_best.max())
    return best_h, best_max


def apply_delta(h, C, p, delta, idx):
    M = idx["M"]
    n = len(h)
    old = h[p]
    if p > 0:
        left = h[:p]
        idxp = idx["left_pos"][p]
        idxn = idx["left_neg"][p]
        C[idxp] += delta * (1.0 - left)
        C[idxn] -= delta * left
    if p + 1 < n:
        right = h[p + 1:]
        idxp = idx["right_pos"][p]
        idxn = idx["right_neg"][p]
        C[idxp] -= delta * right
        C[idxn] += delta * (1.0 - right)
    new = old + delta
    C[M] += new * (1.0 - new) - old * (1.0 - old)
    h[p] = new


def top_score(C, max_val, tol=2.0):
    thr = max(0.0, float(max_val) - tol)
    return float(np.sum(np.maximum(C - thr, 0.0)))


def continuous_local_search(h_init, n, rng, timeout, idx=None, eps_start=0.15):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.asarray(h_init, dtype=np.float64).copy(), 0.0

    if idx is None:
        idx = build_idx_cache(n)
    M = idx["M"]

    h = make_feasible_float(np.asarray(h_init, dtype=np.float64).copy(), n)
    C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.float64)

    cur_max = float(C.max())
    cur_score = top_score(C, cur_max, 2.0)

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_max = cur_max

    eps = float(eps_start)
    no_improve = 0
    stall_limit = 2500
    start_t = time.time()
    trial = 0
    max_trials = 1000000

    while trial < max_trials and (time.time() - start_t) < timeout:
        trial += 1

        max_idx = int(C.argmax())
        cur_max = float(C[max_idx])
        d = max_idx - M
        move = None

        if rng.random_sample() < 0.82 and d != 0:
            if d > 0:
                contrib = h[d:] * (1.0 - h[:n - d])
                cmax = float(contrib.max())
                if cmax > 1e-12:
                    th = 0.5 * cmax
                    pos = np.where(contrib > th)[0]
                    if len(pos) > 0:
                        k = int(pos[rng.randint(len(pos))])
                        src = k + d
                        tgt = k
                        move = (src, tgt)
            else:
                dd = -d
                contrib = h[:n - dd] * (1.0 - h[dd:])
                cmax = float(contrib.max())
                if cmax > 1e-12:
                    th = 0.5 * cmax
                    pos = np.where(contrib > th)[0]
                    if len(pos) > 0:
                        k = int(pos[rng.randint(len(pos))])
                        src = k
                        tgt = k + dd
                        move = (src, tgt)

        if move is None:
            for _try in range(12):
                s = int(rng.randint(n))
                t = int(rng.randint(n))
                if s == t:
                    continue
                if h[s] <= 1e-6 or h[t] >= 1.0 - 1e-6:
                    continue
                if h[s] < h[t]:
                    s, t = t, s
                move = (s, t)
                break

        if move is None:
            break

        src, tgt = move
        e = min(eps, float(h[src]), 1.0 - float(h[tgt]))
        if e <= 1e-9:
            continue

        apply_delta(h, C, src, -e, idx)
        apply_delta(h, C, tgt, e, idx)

        new_max_idx = int(C.argmax())
        new_max = float(C[new_max_idx])

        accept = False
        progress = False
        new_score = cur_score

        if new_max < cur_max - 1e-10:
            accept = True
            progress = True
            new_score = top_score(C, new_max, 2.0)
        elif new_max <= cur_max + 1e-8:
            new_score = top_score(C, new_max, 2.0)
            if new_score < cur_score - 1e-10:
                accept = True
                progress = True
            elif abs(new_score - cur_score) <= 1e-10 and rng.random_sample() < 0.015:
                accept = True
                progress = False

        if accept:
            cur_max = new_max
            cur_score = new_score
            if new_max < best_max - 1e-12:
                best_max = new_max
                best_h = h.copy()
            if progress:
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
        else:
            apply_delta(h, C, src, e, idx)
            apply_delta(h, C, tgt, -e, idx)
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve >= stall_limit:
            eps = max(0.004, eps * 0.72)
            no_improve = 0
            h = make_feasible_float(h, n)
            C = recompute_C(h, dtype=np.float64)
            cur_max = float(C.max())
            cur_score = top_score(C, cur_max, 2.0)

    best_h = make_feasible_float(best_h, n)
    C_best = recompute_C(best_h, dtype=np.float64)
    best_max = float(C_best.max())
    return best_h, best_max


def c5_by_correlate(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(h)
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(corr) * (2.0 / n))


def finalize_solution(h, n):
    if h is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    arr = np.asarray(h)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr.astype(np.float64), 0.0
    if np.issubdtype(arr.dtype, np.integer):
        out = arr.astype(np.float64)
    else:
        out = make_feasible_float(arr.astype(np.float64).copy(), n)
    val = c5_by_correlate(out)
    return out, float(val)


def process_n(n, inits, rng, time_limit):
    if n <= 0:
        return None, float("inf")
    if len(inits) == 0:
        inits = [balance_binary(thue_morse_binary(n), n, rng)]

    best_h = None
    best_c5 = float("inf")

    for h0 in inits:
        hb = balance_binary(h0, n, rng)
        C0 = recompute_C(hb, dtype=np.int32)
        c0 = float(C0.max()) * (2.0 / n)
        if c0 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = c0
            best_h = hb.copy()

    if best_h is None:
        best_h = balance_binary(thue_morse_binary(n), n, rng)
        C0 = recompute_C(best_h, dtype=np.int32)
        best_c5 = float(C0.max()) * (2.0 / n)

    if time_limit < 60.0:
        return finalize_solution(best_h, n)

    idx = build_idx_cache(n)
    total_start = time.time()
    reserve = min(120.0, max(30.0, 0.15 * float(time_limit)))
    local_limit = max(0.0, float(time_limit) - reserve)

    i = 0
    no_global = 0
    best_local_c5 = best_c5

    while (time.time() - total_start) < local_limit:
        rem = local_limit - (time.time() - total_start)
        if rem < 20.0:
            break

        if i == 0:
            seed = best_h
            seg = min(120.0, rem, max(30.0, 0.35 * local_limit))
        else:
            if no_global > 3:
                seed = best_h
                seg = min(60.0, rem)
            else:
                seed = inits[(i - 1) % len(inits)]
                seg = min(45.0, max(15.0, rem / max(1, len(inits))))

        if seg <= 5.0:
            seg = min(20.0, rem)
        if seg <= 0.0:
            break

        h_s, max_count = binary_local_search(seed, n, rng, timeout=seg, idx=idx)
        c_s = float(max_count) * (2.0 / n)
        if c_s < best_local_c5 - 1e-12:
            best_local_c5 = c_s
            best_h = h_s.copy()
            best_c5 = c_s
            no_global = 0
        else:
            no_global += 1
        i += 1

    cont_start = time.time()
    cont_rem = float(time_limit) - (cont_start - total_start) - 5.0
    if cont_rem > 20.0:
        h_cont, max_cont = continuous_local_search(
            best_h, n, rng, timeout=cont_rem, idx=idx, eps_start=0.18
        )
        c_cont = float(max_cont) * (2.0 / n)
        if c_cont < best_c5 - 1e-12:
            best_c5 = c_cont
            best_h = h_cont

    return finalize_solution(best_h, n)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    t0 = time.time()
    if budget_s is None or budget_s <= 0:
        budget_s = 1300.0

    h_parent = None
    try:
        hp_raw = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=np.float64)
        if hp_raw.size > 0:
            h_parent = hp_raw.copy()
    except Exception:
        h_parent = None

    best_h = None
    best_c5 = float("inf")

    if h_parent is not None:
        hp, cpar = finalize_solution(h_parent, len(h_parent))
        if cpar < best_c5:
            best_h = hp
            best_c5 = cpar

    def remaining():
        return max(0.0, float(budget_s) - (time.time() - t0))

    # Fast large-grid scans: useful because a persistent Markov sequence at high n
    # can already be strong without local search.
    if remaining() > 550.0:
        inits = create_inits(1024, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(1024, inits, rng, min(25.0, remaining() - 40.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    if remaining() > 500.0:
        inits = create_inits(2048, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(2048, inits, rng, min(25.0, remaining() - 40.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    if remaining() > 200.0:
        inits = create_inits(4096, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(4096, inits, rng, min(25.0, remaining() - 40.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    # Main local-search phase at a resolution that is easy to polish.
    if remaining() > 220.0:
        inits = create_inits(512, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(512, inits, rng, min(720.0, remaining() - 80.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    # Larger-resolution local search.
    if remaining() > 180.0:
        inits = create_inits(1024, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(1024, inits, rng, min(300.0, remaining() - 80.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    # Smaller-resolution phase can produce an integer count below the target.
    if remaining() > 130.0:
        inits = create_inits(256, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(256, inits, rng, min(220.0, remaining() - 50.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    # Extra polish for the most promising resolution if time remains.
    if remaining() > 170.0:
        inits = create_inits(512, rng, h_parent)
        hn, cn = process_n(512, inits, rng, min(180.0, remaining() - 30.0))
        if hn is not None and cn < best_c5:
            best_h, best_c5 = hn, cn

    if best_h is None:
        n = 256
        hh = balance_binary(thue_morse_binary(n), n, rng).astype(np.float64)
        best_c5 = c5_by_correlate(hh)
        return hh, best_c5, n

    best_h, best_c5 = finalize_solution(best_h, int(len(best_h)))
    return best_h, float(best_c5), int(len(best_h))
```
